<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Special_libraries">Special libraries</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Library&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Special libraries"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Special_library" title="Special library">Special library</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:20170420_Beinecke_Rare_Book_Library_Interior_Yale_University_New_Haven_Connecticut.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/20170420_Beinecke_Rare_Book_Library_Interior_Yale_University_New_Haven_Connecticut.jpg/250px-20170420_Beinecke_Rare_Book_Library_Interior_Yale_University_New_Haven_Connecticut.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="203" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/20170420_Beinecke_Rare_Book_Library_Interior_Yale_University_New_Haven_Connecticut.jpg/500px-20170420_Beinecke_Rare_Book_Library_Interior_Yale_University_New_Haven_Connecticut.jpg 1.5x" data-file-width="4035" data-file-height="3270" /></a><figcaption>Bookshelves at the <a href="/wiki/Beinecke_Rare_Book_%26_Manuscript_Library" class="mw-redirect" title="Beinecke Rare Book &amp; Manuscript Library">Beinecke Rare Book &amp; Manuscript Library</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Many private businesses and public organizations, including hospitals, churches, museums, research laboratories, law firms, and many government departments and agencies, maintain their own libraries for the use of their employees in doing specialized research related to their work. Depending on the particular institution, special libraries may or may not be accessible to the general public or elements thereof.
</p><p>In more specialized institutions such as law firms and research laboratories, librarians employed in special libraries are commonly specialists in the institution's field rather than generally trained librarians, and often are not required to have advanced degrees in a specifically library-related field due to the specialized content and clientele of the library.
</p><p>Special libraries can also include women's libraries or LGBTQ libraries, which serve the needs of women and the LGBTQ community. <a href="/wiki/Libraries_and_the_LGBTQ_community" title="Libraries and the LGBTQ community">Libraries and the LGBTQ community</a> have an extensive history, and there are currently many libraries, archives, and special collections devoted to preserving and helping the LGBTQ community. Women's libraries, such as the Vancouver Women's Library or the <a href="/wiki/Women%27s_Library" title="Women&#39;s Library">Women's Library @LSE</a> are examples of women's libraries that offer services to women and girls and focus on women's history.
</p><p>Some special libraries, such as governmental law libraries, hospital libraries, and military base libraries commonly are open to public visitors to the institution in question. Depending on the particular library and the clientele it serves, special libraries may offer services similar to research, reference, public, academic, or children's libraries, often with restrictions such as only lending books to patients at a hospital or restricting the public from parts of a military collection. Given the highly individual nature of special libraries, visitors to a special library are often advised to check what services and restrictions apply at that particular library.
</p><p>Special libraries are distinguished from <a href="/wiki/Special_collections" title="Special collections">special collections</a>, which are branches or parts of a library intended for rare books, manuscripts, and other special materials, though some special libraries have special collections of their own, typically related to the library's specialized subject area.
</p><p>For more information on specific types of special libraries, see <a href="/wiki/Law_library" title="Law library">law libraries</a>, <a href="/wiki/Medical_library" title="Medical library">medical libraries</a>, <a href="/wiki/Music_library" title="Music library">music libraries</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Transportation_libraries" class="mw-redirect" title="Transportation libraries">transportation libraries</a>.
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Library_makerspaces">Library makerspaces</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Library&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Library makerspaces"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Maker_culture" title="Maker culture">Maker culture</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_open-source_hardware_projects" title="List of open-source hardware projects">List of open-source hardware projects</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_free_and_open-source_software_packages" title="List of free and open-source software packages">List of free and open-source software packages</a>, <a href="/wiki/Category:Graphics_software" title="Category:Graphics software">Lists of graphics software</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_educational_software" title="List of educational software">List of educational software</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_online_educational_resources" title="List of online educational resources">List of online educational resources</a>, <a href="/wiki/Workstation#GPU_workstations" title="Workstation">GPU workstations</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Skill_sharing" class="mw-redirect" title="Skill sharing">Skill sharing</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Fab_lab_in_library.webp" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Fab_lab_in_library.webp/250px-Fab_lab_in_library.webp.png" decoding="async" width="250" height="176" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/3d/Fab_lab_in_library.webp/500px-Fab_lab_in_library.webp.png 1.5x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="720" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Fab_lab" title="Fab lab">Fab lab</a> in a library</figcaption></figure>
<p>A <a href="/wiki/Library_makerspace" title="Library makerspace">library makerspace</a> is a <a href="/wiki/Creativity" title="Creativity">creative</a>, hands-on <a href="/wiki/Makerspace" class="mw-redirect" title="Makerspace">makerspace</a> located within a library that provides tools, technology, and spaces for patrons to <a href="/wiki/Design" title="Design">design</a>, <a href="/wiki/Prototype" title="Prototype">prototype</a>, and create projects. Some makerspaces in libraries could include a <a href="/wiki/Photographic_studio" title="Photographic studio">photographic studio</a>, <a href="/wiki/Recording_studio" title="Recording studio">digital recording studio</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sewing" title="Sewing">sewing room</a>, <a href="/wiki/Computer_lab" title="Computer lab">computer lab</a> <a href="/wiki/Cubicle" title="Cubicle">cubicles</a>, <a href="/wiki/Woodworking" title="Woodworking">woodshop</a> / <a href="/wiki/Machine_shop" title="Machine shop">machine shop</a> and other <a href="/wiki/Household_hardware" title="Household hardware">hardware</a> equipment spaces or <a href="/wiki/Fab_lab" title="Fab lab">fab labs</a> for <a href="/wiki/3D_printing" title="3D printing">3D printers</a>, <a href="/wiki/CNC_machine" class="mw-redirect" title="CNC machine">CNC machines</a>, <a href="/wiki/Laser_cutting" title="Laser cutting">laser cutters</a>, <a href="/wiki/Laser_engraving" title="Laser engraving">laser engraving</a>, <a href="/wiki/Injection_molding_machine" title="Injection molding machine">injection molding machines</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sewing" title="Sewing">sewing</a> machines, <a href="/wiki/Digital_electronics" title="Digital electronics">electronics</a> and <a href="/wiki/Electronic_kit" title="Electronic kit">electronic kits</a>. They may also offer software for <a href="/wiki/Graphics_software#See_also" title="Graphics software">graphic design</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_video_editing_software" title="List of video editing software">video editing</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_3D_modeling_software" title="List of 3D modeling software">3D modeling</a> \ <a href="/wiki/List_of_3D_rendering_software" title="List of 3D rendering software">3D rendering</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Programmer#Programming_education" title="Programmer">computer programming educational</a> resources.  Library makerspaces aim to expand the traditional role of libraries from primarily providing access to <a href="/wiki/Books" class="mw-redirect" title="Books">books</a> and <a href="/wiki/Information" title="Information">information</a> toward enhancing <a href="/wiki/Hard_skills" class="mw-redirect" title="Hard skills">hard skills</a> and enabling active <a href="/wiki/Learning_by_doing" class="mw-redirect" title="Learning by doing">learning by doing</a>. These spaces support <a href="/wiki/STEM" class="mw-redirect" title="STEM">STEM</a> and <a href="/wiki/STEAM_education" title="STEAM education">STEAM education</a>, <a href="/wiki/Digital_literacy" title="Digital literacy">digital literacy</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Lifelong_learning" title="Lifelong learning">lifelong learning</a> for all ages. Activities can range from <a href="/wiki/Code_Club" title="Code Club">code clubs</a>, <a href="/wiki/Robotics" title="Robotics">robotics</a> clubs to <a href="/wiki/Art" title="Art">arts</a>, <a href="/wiki/Craft" title="Craft">crafts</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Multimedia" title="Multimedia">multimedia</a> production.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>79<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>80<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>81<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Associations">Associations</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Library&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Associations"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1320445320" /><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_library_associations" title="List of library associations">List of library associations</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/International_Federation_of_Library_Associations_and_Institutions" title="International Federation of Library Associations and Institutions">International Federation of Library Associations and Institutions</a> (IFLA) is the leading international association of library organizations. It is the global voice of the library and information profession, and its annual conference provides a venue for librarians to learn from one another.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>82<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p><p>Library associations in Asia include the <a href="/wiki/Indian_Library_Association" title="Indian Library Association">Indian Library Association</a> (ILA),<sup id="cite&#95;ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>83<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Indian Association of Special Libraries and Information Centers (IASLIC),<sup id="cite&#95;ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>84<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> Bengal Library Association (BLA), Kolkata,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>85<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Pakistan_Library_Association" title="Pakistan Library Association">Pakistan Library Association</a>,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>86<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> the Pakistan Librarians Welfare Organization,<sup id="cite&#95;ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>87<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> the Bangladesh Association of Librarians, Information Scientists and Documentalists, the Library Association of Bangladesh, and the <a href="/wiki/Sri_Lanka_Library_Association" title="Sri Lanka Library Association">Sri Lanka Library Association</a> (founded 1960).
</p><p>National associations of the English-speaking world include the <a href="/wiki/American_Library_Association" title="American Library Association">American Library Association</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Australian_Library_and_Information_Association" title="Australian Library and Information Association">Australian Library and Information Association</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Canadian_Library_Association" title="Canadian Library Association">Canadian Library Association</a>, the <a href="/wiki/LIANZA" class="mw-redirect" title="LIANZA">Library and Information Association of New Zealand Aotearoa</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Research_Libraries_UK" title="Research Libraries UK">Research Libraries UK</a> (a consortium of 30 university and other research libraries in the United Kingdom). Library bodies such as <a href="/wiki/Chartered_Institute_of_Library_and_Information_Professionals" title="Chartered Institute of Library and Information Professionals">CILIP</a> (formerly the Library Association, founded 1877) may advocate the role that libraries and librarians can play in a modern Internet environment, and in the teaching of <a href="/wiki/Information_literacy" title="Information literacy">information literacy</a> skills.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-CILIP(2010)&#95;41-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CILIP(2010)-41"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>40<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-Rowlandsetal.(2008)&#95;89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Rowlandsetal.(2008)-89"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>88<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Nigerian_Library_Association" title="Nigerian Library Association">Nigerian Library Association</a> is the recognized group for librarians working in Nigeria and it was founded in 1962.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Public_library_advocacy" title="Public library advocacy">Public library advocacy</a> is support given to a public library for its financial and philosophical goals or needs. Most often this takes the form of monetary or material donations or campaigning to the institutions which oversee the library, sometimes by advocacy groups such as <a href="/wiki/Friends_of_Libraries" title="Friends of Libraries">Friends of Libraries</a> and community members. Originally, library advocacy was centered on the library itself, but current trends show libraries positioning themselves to demonstrate they provide "economic value to the community" in means that are not directly related to the checking out of books and other media.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>89<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
<div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Protection">Protection</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Library&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Protection"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p>Libraries are considered part of the cultural heritage and are one of the primary objectives in many state and domestic conflicts and are at risk of destruction and looting. Financing is often carried out by robbing valuable library items. National and international coordination regarding military and civil structures for the protection of libraries is operated by <a href="/wiki/Blue_Shield_International" title="Blue Shield International">Blue Shield International</a> and <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a>. From an international perspective, despite the partial dissolution of state structures and very unclear security situations as a result of the wars and unrest, robust undertakings to protect libraries are being carried out. The topic is also the creation of "no-strike lists", in which the coordinates of important cultural monuments such as libraries have been preserved.<sup id="cite&#95;ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>90<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>91<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>92<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup><sup id="cite&#95;ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94"><span class="cite-bracket">&#91;</span>93<span class="cite-bracket">&#93;</span></a></sup>
</p>
